=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
The definition of an 11/22 string in this problem is the same as in Problems A and C.

A string T is called an 11/22 string when it satisfies all of the following conditions:

- |T| is odd. Here, |T| denotes the length of T.
- The 1-st through (\frac{|T|+1}{2} - 1)-th characters are all 1.
- The (\frac{|T|+1}{2})-th character is /.
- The (\frac{|T|+1}{2} + 1)-th through |T|-th characters are all 2.

For example, 11/22, 111/222, and / are 11/22 strings, but 1122, 1/22, 11/2222, 22/11, and //2/2/211 are not.
Given a string S of length N consisting of 1, 2, and /, process Q queries.
Each query provides two integers L and R. Let T be the (contiguous) substring of S from the L-th through R-th character. Find the maximum length of a subsequence (not necessarily contiguous) of T that is an 11/22 string. If no such subsequence exists, print 0.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format. Here, \mathrm{query}_i denotes the i-th query.
N Q
S
\mathrm{query}_1
\mathrm{query}_2
\vdots
\mathrm{query}_Q

Each query is given in the following format:
L R

Output

Print Q lines. The i-th line should contain the answer to the i-th query.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 10^5
- 1 \leq Q \leq 10^5
- S is a string of length N consisting of 1, 2, and /.
- 1 \leq L \leq R \leq N
- N, Q, L, and R are integers.

Sample Input 1

12 5
111/212/1122
1 7
9 12
3 6
4 10
1 12

Sample Output 1

5
0
3
1
7

For the first query, the substring from the 1-st to 7-th character of S is 111/212. This string contains 11/22 as a subsequence, which is the longest subsequence that is an 11/22 string. Therefore, the answer is 5.
For the second query, the substring from the 9-th to 12-th character of S is 1122. This string does not contain any subsequence that is an 11/22 string, so the answer is 0.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Need implement segment tree:
size = 1
while size < M: size <<=1
seg_max = [ -10**18 ] * (2*size)
seg_min = [ 10**18 ] * (2*size)
for i in range(M):
    seg_max[size+i] = A_vals[i]
    seg_min[size+i] = B_vals[i]
for i in range(size-1,0,-1):
    seg_max[i] = max(seg_max[2*i], seg_max[2*i+1])
    seg_min[i] = min(seg_min[2*i], seg_min[2*i+1])

Range query inclusive l,r 0-based:
def query_max(l,r):
    l += size; r += size
    res = -INF
    while l <= r:
        if l & 1: res=max(res, seg_max[l]); l +=1
        if not (r & 1): res=max(res, seg_max[r]); r -=1
        l >>=1; r >>=1
    return res
Similarly min.

Thus algorithm:
1. Read N,Q,S.
2. Build pref1, pref2 length N+1.
3. Build arrays pos, a_vals, b_vals, s_vals for each slash.
4. For each query:
   A = pref1[L-1]; C = pref2[R]; T=A+C.
   left = bisect_left(pos, L)
   right = bisect_right(pos, R) - 1
   if left > right: output 0
   else:
      idxT = bisect_right(s_vals, T) - 1
      split = idxT
      if split > right: split = right
      k = -1
      if split >= left:
          cand = a_vals[split] - A
          if cand > k: k = cand
      l2 = split + 1
      if l2 < left: l2 = left
      if l2 <= right:
          cand = C - b_vals[l2]
          if cand > k: k = cand
      # if k still -1? Shouldn't. But maybe if split<left and l2>right? If split<left, l2<=left<=right? If left<=right, l2<=right? If split=left-1, l2=left<=right yes. If split<left-1, l2<left, set to left<=right. So candidate2 exists. If split>=right, candidate1 exists. So k>=0.
      ans = 2*k + 1
      output ans

Let's think about proof of s threshold:
For each slash p, define x_p(L) = a_p - A, y_p(R) = C - b_p.
x_p is nondecreasing with p, y_p is nonincreasing with p. The maximum of min(x_p,y_p) over p in interval occurs at the crossing of x and y. Since x_p - y_p = s_p - T, and s_p nondecreasing, the sign changes once. Therefore the maximum is either at last p with s<=T (where x<=y) or first p with s>T (where x>y). This is even clearer. We can mention in comments? Not needed but code can reflect.

    k2 = C - b_vals[idxT + 1]
    k = k1 if k1 > k2 else k2
out.append(str(2*k+1))

Let's ensure code is syntactically correct:
import sys
from bisect import bisect_left, bisect_right

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N, Q = map(int, input().split())
    S = input().strip()
    pref1 = [0] * (N + 1)
    pref2 = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i, ch in enumerate(S, 1):
        pref1[i] = pref1[i-1] + (1 if ch == '1' else 0)
        pref2[i] = pref2[i-1] + (1 if ch == '2' else 0)
    pos = []
    a_vals = []
    b_vals = []
    s_vals = []
    for i, ch in enumerate(S, 1):
        if ch == '/':
            a = pref1[i-1]
            b = pref2[i]  # same as pref2[i-1]
            pos.append(i)
            a_vals.append(a)
            b_vals.append(b)
            s_vals.append(a + b)
    out = []
    for _ in range(Q):
        L, R = map(int, input().split())
        left = bisect_left(pos, L)
        right = bisect_right(pos, R) - 1
        if left > right:
            out.append("0")
            continue
        A = pref1[L-1]
        C = pref2[R]
        T = A + C
        idxT = bisect_right(s_vals, T) - 1
        if idxT < left:
            k = C - b_vals[left]
        elif idxT >= right:
            k = a_vals[right] - A
        else:
            k1 = a_vals[idxT] - A
            k2 = C - b_vals[idxT + 1]
            k = k1 if k1 > k2 else k2
        out.append(str(2 * k + 1))
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

    pref2[i] = pref2[i-1]
    if ch == '1': pref1[i] += 1
    elif ch == '2': pref2[i] += 1
This avoids bool addition. Fine.

Code:
```
import sys
from bisect import bisect_left, bisect_right

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    N = int(data[0])
    Q = int(data[1])
    S = data[2].decode()

    pref1 = [0] * (N + 1)
    pref2 = [0] * (N + 1)
    for i, ch in enumerate(S, 1):
        pref1[i] = pref1[i - 1]
        pref2[i] = pref2[i - 1]
        if ch == '1':
            pref1[i] += 1
        elif ch == '2':
            pref2[i] += 1

    pos = []
    a_vals = []
    b_vals = []
    s_vals = []

    for i, ch in enumerate(S, 1):
        if ch == '/':
            a = pref1[i - 1]
            b = pref2[i]  # same as pref2[i-1] because S[i-1] == '/'
            pos.append(i)
            a_vals.append(a)
            b_vals.append(b)
            s_vals.append(a + b)

    out = []
    idx = 3
    for _ in range(Q):
        L = int(data[idx])
        R = int(data[idx + 1])
        idx += 2

        left = bisect_left(pos, L)
        right = bisect_right(pos, R) - 1

        if left > right:
            out.append("0")
            continue

        A = pref1[L - 1]
        C = pref2[R]
        T = A + C

        # Last slash with a + b <= T
        mid = bisect_right(s_vals, T) - 1

        if mid < left:
            k = C - b_vals[left]
        elif mid >= right:
            k = a_vals[right] - A
        else:
            k1 = a_vals[mid] - A
            k2 = C - b_vals[mid + 1]
            k = k1 if k1 > k2 else k2

        out.append(str(2 * k + 1))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

If `mid` between, `mid+1` valid because mid < right. Good.
If `mid` = -1 and left=0, mid<left, k=C-b[0]. Good.
If `mid` = M-1 and right=M-1, mid>=right, k=a[right]-A. Good.
If `mid` = M-1 and right < M-1, mid>=right, k=a[right]-A. But if mid>=right, all slashes in range have s<=T? Since mid is last global s<=T, if mid>=right, yes all indices <=right have s<=T. Good.

import sys
from bisect import bisect_left, bisect_right

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    N = int(data[0])
    Q = int(data[1])
    S = data[2].decode()

    pref1 = [0] * (N + 1)
    pref2 = [0] * (N + 1)

    for i, ch in enumerate(S, 1):
        pref1[i] = pref1[i - 1]
        pref2[i] = pref2[i - 1]
        if ch == '1':
            pref1[i] += 1
        elif ch == '2':
            pref2[i] += 1

    pos = []
    a_vals = []
    b_vals = []
    s_vals = []

    for i, ch in enumerate(S, 1):
        if ch == '/':
            a = pref1[i - 1]
            b = pref2[i]
            pos.append(i)
            a_vals.append(a)
            b_vals.append(b)
            s_vals.append(a + b)

    out = []
    idx = 3

    for _ in range(Q):
        L = int(data[idx])
        R = int(data[idx + 1])
        idx += 2

        left = bisect_left(pos, L)
        right = bisect_right(pos, R) - 1

        if left > right:
            out.append("0")
            continue

        A = pref1[L - 1]
        C = pref2[R]
        T = A + C

        mid = bisect_right(s_vals, T) - 1

        if mid < left:
            k = C - b_vals[left]
        elif mid >= right:
            k = a_vals[right] - A
        else:
            k1 = a_vals[mid] - A
            k2 = C - b_vals[mid + 1]
            k = k1 if k1 > k2 else k2

        out.append(str(2 * k + 1))

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
